{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\scripts\\student-work.ts", "content": "/**\n * What has a student actually done on LSAT U.\n *\n * The read half of the assign loop: `scripts/assign.ts` puts work into an\n * account, this reports back what came of it. Signs in as the admin and reads\n * under normal admin RLS — the same visibility the Teacher Desk has.\n *\n *   npx vite-node scripts/student-work.ts -- --student \"Celia\"\n *   npx vite-node scripts/student-work.ts -- --student \"Steven Morris\" --days 30\n */\nimport fs from \"node:fs\";\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\n\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \"..\");\n\nfunction readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {\n  const out: Record<string, string> = {};\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, \"utf8\").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n    const t = line.trim();\n    if (!t || t.startsWith(\"#\")) continue;\n    const i = t.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (i < 0) continue;\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g, \"\");\n  }\n  return out;\n}\nconst env = {\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, \"../../.env\")),\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, \".env\")),\n};\n\nfunction arg(n: string) {\n  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${n}`);\n  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;\n}\nconst query = arg(\"student\");\nconst days = Number(arg(\"days\") ?? 30);\nif (!query) {\n  console.error('usage: vite-node scripts/student-work.ts -- --student <name|email> [--days 30]');\n  process.exit(2);\n}\n\nconst since = new Date(Date.now() - days * 86400_000).toISOString();\nconst d = (s: string | null) => (s ? s.slice(0, 10) : \"—\");\n\nasync function main() {\n  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({\n    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,\n    password: [REDACTED],\n  });\n  if (authErr) {\n    console.error(`sign-in failed: ${authErr.message}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  const { data: users, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke(\"admin-manage-users\", {\n    method: \"GET\",\n  });\n  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);\n\n  const q = query!.trim().toLowerCase();\n  const rows = (users ?? []) as { class_id: string; display_name: string | null; email: string | null }[];\n  const exact = rows.filter((r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q);\n  const pool = exact.length\n    ? exact\n    : rows.filter((r) => r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q));\n\n  if (pool.length !== 1) {\n    console.error(pool.length ? `\"${query}\" is ambiguous:` : `no student matches \"${query}\".`);\n    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.display_name ?? \"(no name)\"} <${r.email ?? \"?\"}>`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n  const s = pool[0];\n  const id = s.class_id;\n\n  console.log(`\\n${s.display_name ?? \"(no name)\"}  <${s.email ?? \"?\"}>`);\n  console.log(`last ${days} days\\n${\"─\".repeat(60)}`);\n\n  // Activities\n  const { data: acts } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activity_assignments\")\n    .select(\"id,title,status,mode,created_at\")\n    .eq(\"student_id\", id)\n    .order(\"created_at\", { ascending: false });\n  console.log(`\\nACTIVITIES (${acts?.length ?? 0})`);\n  for (const a of acts ?? []) {\n    console.log(`  ${d(a.created_at)}  ${String(a.status).padEnd(12)} ${a.title}`);\n  }\n\n  // Read, Recall, Colour grades\n  const { data: grades } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activity_section_grades\")\n    .select(\"source_section,attempt_no,carried_pct,stimuli_graded,created_at\")\n    .eq(\"student_id\", id)\n    .order(\"created_at\", { ascending: false });\n  if (grades?.length) {\n    console.log(`\\nREAD, RECALL, COLOUR — graded runs (${grades.length})`);\n    for (const g of grades) {\n      const pct = g.carried_pct == null ? \"  —\" : `${String(Math.round(Number(g.carried_pct))).padStart(3)}%`;\n      console.log(`  ${d(g.created_at)}  ${pct} carried   ${g.source_section ?? \"?\"}  attempt ${g.attempt_no ?? \"?\"}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Guided drills\n  const { data: drills } = await supabase\n    .from(\"guided_drill_assignments\")\n    .select(\"title,status,created_at\")\n    .eq(\"student_id\", id)\n    .order(\"created_at\", { ascending: false })\n    .limit(15);\n  console.log(`\\nGUIDED DRILLS (${drills?.length ?? 0} most recent)`);\n  for (const g of drills ?? []) {\n    console.log(`  ${d(g.created_at)}  ${String(g.status).padEnd(12)} ${g.title}`);\n  }\n\n  // Question-bank work\n  const { count: attempts } = await supabase\n    .from(\"attempts\")\n    .select(\"*\", { count: \"exact\", head: true })\n    .eq(\"user_id\", id)\n    .gte(\"created_at\", since);\n  const { count: correct } = await supabase\n    .from(\"attempts\")\n    .select(\"*\", { count: \"exact\", head: true })\n    .eq(\"user_id\", id)\n    .eq(\"is_correct\", true)\n    .gte(\"created_at\", since);\n  const pct = attempts ? Math.round((100 * (correct ?? 0)) / attempts) : null;\n  console.log(\n    `\\nQUESTIONS (last ${days}d): ${attempts ?? 0} attempted` +\n      (pct === null ? \"\" : `, ${correct} correct — ${pct}%`),\n  );\n\n  // Latest messages both ways\n  const { data: convs } = await supabase\n    .from(\"conversation_participants\")\n    .select(\"conversation_id\")\n    .eq(\"user_id\", id);\n  const cids = (convs ?? []).map((c) => c.conversation_id);\n  if (cids.length) {\n    const { data: msgs } = await supabase\n      .from(\"messages\")\n      .select(\"body,sender_id,created_at\")\n      .in(\"conversation_id\", cids)\n      .order(\"created_at\", { ascending: false })\n      .limit(4);\n    if (msgs?.length) {\n      console.log(`\\nLAST MESSAGES`);\n      for (const m of msgs.reverse()) {\n        const who = m.sender_id === id ? \"her\" : \"you\";\n        const line = String(m.body).replace(/\\s+/g, \" \").slice(0, 70);\n        console.log(`  ${d(m.created_at)}  ${who.padEnd(4)} ${line}`);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  console.log(\"\");\n}\n\nmain()\n  .then(() => process.exit(0))\n  .catch((e) => {\n    console.error(e instanceof Error ? e.message : e);\n    process.exit(1);\n  });\n"}

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

## The apology / accountability move

One of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.

> I'm so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn't communicate that as well as I should have.

> I've been away from work for personal reasons, but I'm back now and getting caught up.

The pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.

## Situational micro-templates

Verbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.

- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.
  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don't worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.
- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua's explicit go-ahead.
- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**
  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.
- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.
  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I'm glad we got to talk through where you're at and what you're aiming for on test day.
- **Inbound prospect (hasn't booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.
  > Thanks for reaching out ... I'd be happy to talk more about what you're working on and see if we'd be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.
- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.
  > Please don't apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.

## The three registers

Which voice governs which surface.

## user

Tool result:
### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, recaps, in-drill feedback, email

Fully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.

Reference line (target tone):

> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let's slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You've got this.

- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it's genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.
  > Missing this is expected. It's one of the hardest moves on the test. Let's slow down and see where it slipped.
- **Accountability (student isn't doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never "the reps aren't happening," never tough love.
- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.
  > That's the cleanest you've split premise from conclusion yet. Let's push it into the harder sets.
- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. "Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting."

### 2. Teaching content (earnest substance, wit at the edges) -- bootcamp lessons, drill explanations

The substance stays earnest and clear (you are explaining a hard skill). The wit lives only at the edges: headers, openers, transitions. No jokes mid-explanation, nothing that costs comprehension.

> Header: "The flaw hides in plain sight."
> Body: earnest, clear, step by step.

### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing

Confident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed "the next card is the page in front of you" was clever but empty).

> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.

Brand copy speaks as the platform ("we", "you"), not as an individual ("I", "Joshua"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.

## Don't

- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)
- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.
- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging ("you might want to").
- No hype/startup vocabulary.
- No em dashes, ever.

Related: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).

Read it. Two things are wrong with that sentence, one of them substantive.

**"Intake only"** is clinical and abstract, and it's doing a job the surrounding UI already does. The box right above it says *"Nothing is recording yet."*

**The real problem: "with full intonation" contradicts the step.** Step 1 is silent reading. Your own glossary defines Read as *"The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken."* The older copy said *"hear the sentence naturally in your head,"* which made intonation obviously internal. The live version dropped that, so a student reasonably reads "full intonation" as instruction to speak, one step early. That's a comprehension bug, not just awkward diction.

Also worth noting: your uncommitted rewrite (*"Read about one sentence at a time, paying attention to what it means. If that feels like too much to remember, start with a shorter phrase."*) is much cleaner, but it drops the **why** — that the beginning is what you lose. Your own rule is that the reasoning is the product, so I'd keep it.

Three options, all silent-safe, no em dashes:

**A — closest to your rewrite, reason restored**
> Read about one sentence at a time, silently, paying attention to what it means. If that feels like too much to remember, start with a shorter phrase. The part you lose first is always the beginning.

**B — keeps the "hear it in your head" fix**
> Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

**C — shortest**
> Read one sentence at a time, silently. Take less if you are unsure how much to take. The beginning is what slips first.

I'd ship **A**. It's your own rewrite's rhythm, it fixes the silent/intonation collision, and it keeps the one line that teaches rather than instructs.

Two things to know before we apply it: this has to land in **both** the repo JSON and the database, the way `20260906220000_simplify_recall_read_step.sql` does, or students keep seeing the old text. And Celia's assignment is `in_progress`, not `completed` — so that migration's filter will update her snapshot in place, which is what you want here.

Say which one, or edit A directly, and I'll write the JSON, the migration, and update the test that pins this string.

That bundle would fail to deploy. Reverting it now.

**A correction first.** I said "the RRC drill" many times. This is wrong. In this repo, **Drill** is a reserved word. A Drill is the question-bank engine. It uses multiple-choice questions and a timer. **Read, Recall, Colour is an Activity.** An Activity is not a Drill.

## What you asked for

You asked for three things:

1. Give the **Activity** "Read, Recall, Colour" to your students.
2. Make sure the Activity operates correctly first.
3. Make an easy and repeatable way to do this again.

## What an Activity assignment is

An **Activity assignment** is one student's own copy of an Activity. The system copies the content at the time you assign it. This copy does not change later. It records the student's progress.

## What I did

I examined the code. I examined the live site. I found two defects in the student-facing text:

- The door page showed the title "Recall Rephrase Colour". This name does not exist. The correct name is "Read, Recall, Colour".
- The text for the **Read** step told the student to speak. The Read step is silent. The student speaks in the **Retelling** step, which is the next step.

I corrected both defects. I also wrote your new text for the Read step.

I made two programs:

- `scripts/assign.ts` gives an Activity to a student.
- `scripts/student-work.ts` shows you what a student did.

Both programs use the application's own code. They do not use a second copy of it.

## What is complete

/**
 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
 *
 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order — every step numbered and open,
 * every section of the written instructions on screen — with one Start button
 * at the top and the same one at the foot. Nothing here begins the run.
 */
export function SectionBriefingPage({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  itemCount,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  sourceSection,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];

  const startButton = (label: string) => (
    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
      {label}
      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
    </button>
  );

  return (
    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
      <header className="sl-bp-head">
        <div>
          <span className="sl-chip">
            <Mic size={11} strokeWidth={2.6} aria-hidden="true" />
            Read, Recall, Colour
          </span>
          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
          <p className="sl-tagline">
            {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
          </p>
          <p className="sl-bp-meta">
            {[
              sourceSection,
              typeof itemCount === "number" ? `${itemCount} pieces to read` : null,
              timeGoalMinutes ? `about ${timeGoalMinutes} min` : null,
            ]
              .filter(Boolean)
              .join(" · ")}
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="sl-cta">
          {startButton("Start the section")}
          {onExit && (
            <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={onExit}>
              Back
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </header>

      <p className="sl-notice" role="status">
        Read this through first. Nothing is recording yet — the microphone only
        opens when you press Start, and the whole section is then recorded in
        one take that you cannot pause.
      </p>

      {steps.length > 0 && (
        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
            {steps.map((step, i) => (
              <li key={i}>
                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ol>
        </section>
      )}

      {briefing && (
        <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Full instructions</h2>
          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
            <section key={i}>
              <h4>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h4>
              {section.body.map((para, j) => (
                <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-bp-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>

## assistant

I found root causes for all of it, including the font. Here is the plan.

## What is actually wrong

**1. "22 pieces to read" uses a word that already means something else.**
[SectionBriefingPage.tsx:64](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx:64). Your own instructions say *"Read each piece silently"*, where a **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence. So "22 pieces" reads as 22 sentences. It actually means 22 **stimuli**. The word is doing two jobs at two different grains, which is why it does not parse.

Fix: `PT1.S3 · 22 stimuli · about 25 min`. "Stimulus" is already on screen in the demo card, so it is not new vocabulary. If you want it plainer, `22 arguments` works, but some LR stimuli are not arguments, so it would be slightly false.

**2. The em dash rule exists and is enforced, just not here.**
You already have `no em dash` tests guarding four components: `ActivityIntro`, `SectionLaunch`, `SectionReportCard`, `SectionRunHistory`. `SectionBriefingPage` is new as of yesterday and has no test, so the em dash walked straight in at [line 82](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx:82). There is a second one in `SectionLibrary.tsx:172`.

Fix: remove both, and add the same guard test to the new page so it cannot come back. The rule is in `wiki/teaching-voice.md` already; it is enforcement that is missing, not the rule.

**3. The font problem is a real CSS bug, not taste.**
`.sl-tagline` is `color: #fff` with a text shadow ([section-launch.css:238](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css:238)). That class was written for the door's dark purple hero. The briefing page reuses it on a cream background. White text on cream is what you are seeing.

Fix: give the briefing page its own tagline colour token instead of inheriting the dark hero's white.

**4. The notice block is doing too much.**
It is one long sentence carrying three separate facts: nothing is recording, the mic opens on Start, the section records in one take with no pause. Two of those repeat in Step 4 and in the full instructions.

Fix: cut it to the one thing that lowers anxiety right now, "Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start." Let the rest live where it already lives.

**5. The page says everything twice.**
It renders "What you do, step by step" (4 steps) and then "Full instructions" (6 prose sections covering the same method). That is why it feels complicated: she reads the method, then reads it again, longer, and cannot tell which one is the real instruction.

Fix: the 4 steps are the instruction. Collapse "Full instructions" behind a disclosure, closed by default. Anyone who wants the long version opens it. Hierarchy becomes: title, one line of what this is, the meta line, Start, the 4 steps, then the optional long version.

## Order I would do it in

1. Copy and em dash fixes, plus the guard test. Small, safe, no visual risk.
2. The tagline colour bug. One rule, fixes readability immediately.
3. Collapse the duplicate briefing. This is the real simplification and the only one that changes layout, so it wants a look at the result before it ships.

One caveat worth saying plainly: this page landed in your Lovable session yesterday, so if you keep editing there, my changes and yours will collide the way they nearly did an hour ago. Cleanest is for me to do all three in one commit while you stay off this file, or for you to do them in Lovable and I stay off it.

Want me to start on 1 and 2 now, and show you 3 before it ships?

      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
        <p>When you are ready, press Start. The clock begins counting up and the recording begins.</p>
        <div className="sl-cta">{startButton("Start the section")}</div>
      </footer>
    </div>
  );
}

## Why "Intake only" is still on your screen

Nothing to do with pushing. Three separate walls, and I hit all of them:

1. **Git can't reach it.** That sentence is a row in Postgres. Pushing code never touches data.
2. **I can't write it directly.** I just tried a direct database update. Your auto-mode classifier blocked it, correctly. I'm not going to route around that.
3. **I can't run the migration either.** It has to disable `activity_assignments_guard_immutables` to rewrite already-issued snapshots, and that needs table-owner rights. My admin login can't execute SQL or toggle triggers.

So this one is genuinely yours to run. It is not a code problem and there is nothing further I can push.

## The one action

Supabase dashboard → SQL Editor → paste → Run. About ten seconds.

```sql
do $$
declare
  v_read_step text := 'Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.';
begin
  update public.activities
     set instructions = jsonb_set(instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
   where slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments disable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;

  update public.activity_assignments aa
     set instructions = jsonb_set(aa.instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
    from public.activities a
   where aa.activity_id = a.id
     and a.slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and a.mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and aa.status is distinct from 'completed'
     and aa.instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments enable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;
end $$;
```

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "Admins delete activity assignments" ON public.activity_assignments;
CREATE POLICY "Admins delete activity assignments"
  ON public.activity_assignments FOR DELETE TO authenticated
  USING (public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin'));

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_activity_assignment_immutables()
RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql SET search_path = public AS $fn$
BEGIN
  IF public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin') THEN
    RETURN NEW;
  END IF;
  IF NEW.activity_id   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.activity_id   OR
     NEW.student_id    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.student_id    OR
     NEW.title         IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.title         OR
     NEW.subtitle      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.subtitle      OR
     NEW.instructions  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.instructions  OR
     NEW.item_format   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.item_format   OR
     NEW.items         IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.items         OR
     NEW.assigned_by   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.assigned_by   OR
     NEW.class_id      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.class_id      OR
     NEW.created_at    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.created_at THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'activity_assignments: snapshot columns are immutable for non-admin users';
  END IF;
  RETURN NEW;
END;
$fn$;

## Now, your actual question

**The content exists in your codebase three times, and none of them is wired to the database.**

1. `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` — the authored source, in the repo
2. `activities.instructions` — a JSONB column in Postgres, **what students actually read**
3. `activity_assignments.instructions` — a per-student copy, taken at assign time

The repo JSON is only used by `ActivityLab`, `SectionLibraryPanel`, and tests. Students never see it. They read #2, or #3 once assigned.

**Nothing syncs #1 into #2.** The only path has been hand-written seed migrations. And there's a comment in `read-recall-colour-sections.test.ts` claiming the content is "copied in by the seed builder" — I searched the repo, **that builder does not exist**. It was never written. So every content change has depended on Lovable hand-authoring SQL, and the repo and the database drift apart in between. That's the whole reason today felt broken.

**The snapshot half is right and worth keeping.** If you fix a typo, a student mid-section shouldn't have her instructions shift under her, and a graded run definitely shouldn't. That's why `completed` stayed old.

**The missing half is a content-push command.** One script that reads the authored JSON, writes `activities`, updates open snapshots, skips completed ones, and prints exactly what changed. That is what I just did by hand. Made permanent, your answer to "why isn't this my codebase" becomes: it is, and `npm run content:push` proves it.

Want me to build that as `scripts/content-push.ts` and wire it to an npm script? It's maybe thirty lines, it makes the JSON genuinely authoritative, and it removes the need for a migration every time you reword a sentence.

{"command": "cat > scripts/content-push.ts <<'TSEOF'\n/**\n * Push authored Activity content from this repo into the database.\n *\n * Read, Recall, Colour's briefing is authored once, in\n * `src/features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`.\n * Students never read that file. They read `activities.instructions`, or, once\n * the work is assigned, the per-student copy in\n * `activity_assignments.instructions`.\n *\n * Nothing connected the two. A test in this repo says the briefing is \"copied in\n * by the seed builder\"; no such builder was ever written, so every reworded\n * sentence needed a hand-authored migration, and in between the file and the\n * database simply disagreed. This is that builder.\n *\n *   npx vite-node scripts/content-push.ts -- --dry-run\n *   npx vite-node scripts/content-push.ts\n *\n * Completed assignments are never touched. A run that has been graded keeps the\n * words it was graded under; rewriting them would re-score finished work.\n */\nimport fs from \"node:fs\";\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\n\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \"..\");\nconst SOURCE = path.join(\n  REPO,\n  \"src/features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json\",\n);\n/** Every Activity whose instructions are this one authored briefing. */\nconst SLUG_PREFIX = \"read-recall-colour-\";\n\nfunction readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {\n  const out: Record<string, string> = {};\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, \"utf8\").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n    const t = line.trim();\n    if (!t || t.startsWith(\"#\")) continue;\n    const i = t.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (i < 0) continue;\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g, \"\");\n  }\n  return out;\n}\nconst env = {\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, \"../../.env\")),\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, \".env\")),\n};\n\nconst dryRun = process.argv.includes(\"--dry-run\");\n\n/** Key order differs between Postgres jsonb and the file, so compare sorted. */\nfunction stable(value: unknown): string {\n  return JSON.stringify(value, (_k, v) =>\n    v && typeof v === \"object\" && !Array.isArray(v)\n      ? Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(v as object).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b)))\n      : v,\n  );\n}\n\nasync function main() {\n  const authored = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(SOURCE, \"utf8\"));\n  const want = stable(authored);\n\n  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({\n    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,\n    password: [REDACTED],\n  });\n  if (authErr) {\n    console.error(\n      `content-push: sign-in failed - ${authErr.message}\\n` +\n        \"  LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD live in ~/ai-os/.env,\\n\" +\n        \"  never in this repo's .env, which is tracked in git.\",\n    );\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  console.log(`source: ${path.relative(REPO, SOURCE)}`);\n  console.log(dryRun ? \"mode:   dry run, nothing is written\\n\" : \"mode:   writing\\n\");\n\n  // ---- the catalog ------------------------------------------------------\n  const { data: activities, error } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activities\")\n    .select(\"id,slug,instructions\")\n    .like(\"slug\", `${SLUG_PREFIX}%`)\n    .order(\"slug\");\n  if (error) throw error;\n\n  const staleActivities = (activities ?? **The database is ahead of the repo, not behind.** The JSON file is the stale one. Compare:

| | repo JSON | live database |
|---|---|---|
| lede | "Read these instructions once before your first section." | "Worth reading properly once. After that, skim the rules above and go." |

| rules[1] | "Read roughly one sentence at a time, without saying it aloud…" | "Read silently, about a sentence at a time. When you are not sure how much to take on, take less." |

The database copy is tighter and much more your voice. That's your Lovable editing, which wrote straight to Postgres and never came back to the file. Nearly every field differs, and **the repo has an eighth rule the database dropped**.

Running the push I just built would have overwritten all of it with older, worse text. That's the real cost of the missing sync: it broke in the direction nobody was watching.

So the tool needs to go both ways, and pulling comes first.


I also wired `npm run assign` and `npm run student-work` to the earlier scripts.

**I did not run the push, and you shouldn't either yet.** Here's why, and it's the important part of this whole thread.

The diff says **13 of 13 activities differ**, and not in one field. Nearly every field. That's because the database is the side that's been maintained. Your Lovable edits wrote to Postgres and never came back to the file, so the file is a months-old snapshot. Your live rules, lede and headings are all better than what's in the repo, and the repo still carries an eighth rule the live copy deliberately dropped.

If I had shipped push-only and run it, you'd have silently lost all of that.

So the sequence is:

1. `npm run content:pull` — takes the live copy into the file
2. Read the diff, run the tests, commit it
3. From then on the file is genuinely the source of truth and `content:push` is safe

One thing to decide before pulling: your Read step is currently the option B wording we agreed, live in the database. Pulling brings that back into the file correctly. But it will also overwrite the file's other fields with the live versions, which is the point. Nothing is lost either way, since git has the file's current state.

## What was preventing it, and what's left

**Nothing, for copy. It works now.** Say "fix this" with a screenshot and the loop is:

1. I edit the JSON
2. `npm run content:push` → **live in seconds**
3. commit + push to main → on the record

No migration. No deploy. No SQL editor.

Four things were in the way, and all four are gone:

- **The copy was never in git's reach.** It's a `jsonb` column in Postgres. That's why pushing to main did nothing, and why it felt insane. There was no path from the repo to the thing students read.
- **Nobody knew admins bypass the immutability trigger.** `guard_activity_assignment_immutables` returns early for admins. That's why every wording change had been going through a hand-written migration for months. It never needed to.
- **Your permission classifier blocked database writes.** You turned that off, which is what finally let me write directly.
- **The file and database had drifted so far that pushing was dangerous.** 13 of 13 rows differed. Now they're identical, so push is safe from here on.

I also saved this to memory so a fresh session knows it without you re-explaining.

## The one thing I still can't answer

**Publishing on Lovable.** Copy changes don't need it, they're already live. But **code** changes do, and I don't know whether your Lovable project pulls from GitHub automatically or whether you have to hit deploy. Everything I pushed today, the heading fix, the briefing page fixes, the scripts, is on `main` but may not be deployed.

Check Lovable once and tell me which it is, and I'll write it into the runbook so neither of us guesses again.

## Two live bugs I fixed along the way that you didn't ask about

- **The door was teaching retired vocabulary.** It said *"say it back"* and *"The Read"*. Your `CONTEXT.md` retires both: step 2 is **"Tell it back"**, step 4 is **The Recall**, because "The Read" collided with step 1. The 12 section pages had the correct names; only the door was stale. It now matches.
- **Your repo file was the stale side**, not the database. If I'd built push-only as originally planned and run it, you'd have silently lost months of better copy.

## So, to answer you directly

**Yes. That is the current case.** The loop works end to end today:

1. I push to `main`
2. **Lovable pulls from GitHub on its own** — that's what those two commit cards in your sidebar are, with my commit messages on them
3. It reaches lsatprep.study

You do not have to do anything for code. You did not click publish for these, and they are live.

Two things worth separating, because they behave differently:

- **"Update preview"** in that sidebar only refreshes Lovable's *in-editor* preview. It is not what makes the live site change. That's why the card said "Preview is out of date" while the real site was already correct.
- **Copy changes don't involve any of this.** Those go straight to Postgres via `npm run content:push` and are live in seconds, no commit, no deploy, no Lovable.

## What "fix this" costs now

For wording: I edit the JSON, run `content:push`, commit. Live in about ten seconds.

For code or layout: I edit, run the tests, push to main. Lovable takes it from there.

The one caution I'd keep: you and I both editing the same file will collide, exactly as it nearly did earlier when your 55 Lovable commits landed on files I had open. If you're working in Lovable on something, tell me which surface and I'll stay off it.

Pushed: `ba7a008..4a28e8f`. Lovable will pick it up the way it did the last two.

**The white heading was the same bug you'd already caught once.** `.sl-title` is `color: #fff` at up to 96px, written for the door's dark purple hero, reused on the cream briefing page. I fixed `.sl-tagline` for exactly this reason earlier and didn't check its neighbour. On the briefing page it's now ink-coloured, and 30–46px instead of 46–96px, so it stops overflowing its own line. The italic section half was apricot for the same reason; that's now the softer ink.

**The meta line was saying the section twice.** The title reads "Read, Recall, Colour · PT104 Section 1" and then the line under it repeated "PT104.S1". | `.sl-brief` | the **Back** button | 15px |

All three are hero classes on a paper page. `.sl-title` is already fixed and waiting on deploy. The other two are still live and effectively unreadable. **Back is a button nobody can see.**

## Passing but weak, and the hierarchy is inverted

| Element | Size | Contrast | Problem |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.sl-bp-meta` | 13px | 5.93 | smallest text on the page and the second-faintest |
| `.sl-bp-h2` | 15px | 5.93 | **section headings are fainter and smaller than the body text they introduce** (17px at 8.49) |
| `h4` in the long version | 26px | 16.29 | at 26px it outranks the 15px section heading above it |

That middle row is the real readability problem. "What you do, step by step" and "Read the long version" are the page's structure, and they're currently the *weakest* type on it. Body copy is winning against its own headings.

## What I'm changing

Darken the two invisible ones onto ink, lift the two weak ones to full ink, and bring the long version's `h4` down so section headings outrank the text inside them. Nothing moves; only colour, weight and size.

## assistant

Pushed: `4a28e8f..14c4d5c`. 2,658 tests passing, typecheck clean.

## What changed

**Three invisible things, now readable:**

| | was | now |
|---|---|---|
| `.sl-chip` — the "READ, RECALL, COLOUR" pill | white on cream, 1.11 | ink on paper, ~17 |
| `.sl-brief` — the **Back** button | white on cream, 1.11 | ink-2, ~8.5 |
| `.sl-title` — the heading | white on cream, 1.11 | fixed last commit |

Back was a button nobody could see.

**Two weak things, and the cause was one bug:** `.sl-bp-meta` and `.sl-bp-h2` both asked for `var(--rd-ink-soft)`, **a token this file never defines.** They fell through to a global muted colour and landed at 5.93, fainter than the 8.49 body copy beneath them. Your section headings were losing to their own paragraphs. Both now use real tokens; headings sit at ~16.

**One inverted hierarchy:** the long version's `h4` was 26px under a 15px section heading, so the essay outranked its own label. Now 20px.

## The part worth your attention

That undefined-token failure is the *same* one your `CLAUDE.md` already records: three token names shipped undefined for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button. An unresolved `var()` doesn't throw. It inherits, renders something plausible, and no test notices.

So I added a guard: every `--rd-` token used in that stylesheet must be declared in it, and nothing scoped to the briefing page may paint text white. I checked it isn't vacuous — 24 tokens defined, 22 used across 216 references, 4 scoped rules inspected.

Worth noting the pattern across tonight: **every one of these was found by you looking at the screen, not by the test suite.** 2,658 tests passed the whole time the page had invisible text on it. The two guards I added (em dashes, CSS tokens) close the specific holes, but the general one — "does this actually render legibly" — is still only covered by you noticing.

If you want, the next useful thing is running that contrast audit across the other RRC surfaces, the door and the runner, since they share the same stylesheet and the same reuse pattern.

**Section Launch**:
The page a student lands on before running an RRC section: the method as four Beats, her last few Attempts, and the one control that starts the run. `SectionLaunch` in code, `.sl-` in CSS. It replaces the editorial intro for section mode only; the eight typed Activities keep theirs.
_Avoid_: The Chamber (the approved mockup's name for it, and already a world under `worlds/chamber/`), Homepage (that is an Activity's own intro page), intro, splash, lobby, dashboard

**Beat**:
Internal code term for one of the four method steps shown on Section Launch. It may remain in implementation names, but it is never student-facing. Students see **step**: Read it, Tell it back, Colour it, Recall it.
_Avoid in student-facing copy_: Beat, beats, phase, tab, card

Your glossary already settles this. `CONTEXT.md:122`:

> **Beat**: Internal code term for one of the four method steps shown on Section Launch. It may remain in implementation names, but **it is never student-facing. Students see step**.
> _Avoid in student-facing copy_: Beat, beats, phase, tab, card

So it's a dev word that leaked. Four things are awkward about that sentence:

1. **"Beats" is your internal name for the thing**, banned in student copy by your own glossary.
2. **The page contradicts itself.** Directly below this paragraph the interface labels them **Step 1, Step 2, Step 3, Step 4**. The prose says beats, the UI says steps, about ten pixels apart.
3. **"then a fourth" is orphaned.** A fourth *what*? The noun is three words back, so the reader carries it.
4. **"once you have got all of it"** is ambiguous exactly where it must not be. Read all of it, or remembered all of it? That clause is the trigger for step 4, so it has to be precise.

Fixing it minimally, as you asked, changing only what's wrong:

## assistant

**Live now** (20 database rows updated), and pushed as `3aa08af..f63ad1f`.

> Three **steps** through the stimulus, then a fourth **step** once you have **read** all of it. You decide how much to take on at a time, and nothing on the screen is tracking where you are.

Three changes, nothing else touched:

- **beats → steps**, which is what your glossary prescribes
- **"a fourth" → "a fourth step"**, so the noun isn't three words back
- **"got all of it" → "read all of it"**, because that clause triggers step four and can't be ambiguous between having read it and having remembered it

Refresh and it's there. Copy goes straight to Postgres, so no deploy needed.

## The pattern worth noticing

This is the third time tonight with the same shape:

| Rule | Where it was enforced | Where it leaked |
|---|---|---|
| No em dashes | 4 older surfaces | the new briefing page |
| No "beat"/"intake" | the door | the new briefing page |
| Define your CSS tokens | `CLAUDE.md` | the new briefing page |

The rules were all written down and all correct. `SectionBriefingPage` arrived in yesterday's Lovable session without any of the guards its neighbours carry, so every rule the codebase already had passed straight through it.

The door's vocabulary guard couldn't catch this one even in principle: **the door never renders the written instructions.** Only this page does. So the essay body has never been checked against your glossary by anything until now.

## The tracking file

[wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md](wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md) — every correction from tonight with **the reason it was wrong**, which is the part that stops it recurring. Nine entries, each with the lesson:

- "Recall Rephrase Colour" → the real name — *when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that it matches*
- "Intake only…" → plain instruction — *when copy is trimmed, check what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for*
- "Three beats" → "Three steps" — *never let an implementation name reach the page*
- "22 pieces to read" → removed — *a unit word must mean one thing per surface*
- "say it back" / "The Read" → "tell it back" / "The Recall" — *a rename isn't finished until every row carries it*
- the three-fact notice, the method told twice, the duplicated meta line

Plus a never/instead table at the bottom.

Linked from `teaching-voice.md` so the voice doc points at it, and saved to memory so a fresh session reads it before touching your copy.

## The guard

`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the **words**, not the components. Every surface that renders that file is covered, including ones not written yet. `coach` is exempt, since it's the grader's brief and needs the exact internal vocabulary the rest of the file can't use.

Two things I corrected in my own work while building it:

- I'd banned **"stimuli"**. It isn't on your Avoid list and it's a legitimate LSAT word. A wrong rule in a guard is worse than no rule, because it blocks correct copy later.
- I added a first assertion checking the walk **found copy at all**. A traversal that silently returned nothing would satisfy every other assertion and prove nothing — which is close to how the "Recall Rephrase Colour" test passed for months.

One honest limit: this covers the RRC instructions file. The other eight Activities have their own content and no equivalent guard. Worth pointing the same test at them when you next touch one.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\impeccable

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\impeccable

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.
